# LETTER

## FROM THE TRANSLATOR to one of his friends.

*Here I am, Sir, since you wished it, ranked in the chemical category <term>chemical category: the author refers to the community of "chymists," a term that in this era encompassed both early modern chemistry and the traditional pursuit of alchemy</term>, and as a mark of my obedience, I send you the translation that you so much desired; to tell the truth, I do not expect a very great harvest from it, knowing the taste of the Age <note>original: "le goût du Siecle"; referring to the prevailing intellectual trends of the late 17th or early 18th century</note> as I do, and I am quite sure that people would much rather see treatises of philosophy according to Des-* <note>The text cuts off here, but likely refers to René Descartes, whose mechanical philosophy was then superseding older alchemical and Aristotelian traditions.</note>

<summary>In this opening of the translator's preface, the author presents his work to a friend while expressing doubt about its potential success, noting that the current intellectual fashion favors Cartesian philosophy over "chemical" or alchemical subjects.</summary>
<keywords>preface, alchemy, chemistry, Descartes, 17th-century philosophy, translation</keywords>

--- Page 6 ---
<meta>This page continues the sentence from the previous page, completing the name of René Descartes. The author is contrasting the "modern" mechanical philosophy of Descartes with the "ancient" Hermetic philosophy of Hermes Trismegistus.</meta>

LETTRE.

*cartes than according to Hermes <note>original: "Hermés"; refers to Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Greek-Egyptian figure to whom the Hermetic corpus and alchemical tradition are attributed.</note>. The former is in fashion and possesses all the charms of novelty, whereas the latter is so old and so worn out that his name is scarcely known to the world; one proposes only things that are easy to demonstrate, by remaining solely at the surface of bodies, while the other, more abstract, concerns itself only with the internal essence of things; finally, the one, confining itself to mechanics <term>mechanics: the "mechanical philosophy" of the 17th century, which viewed the universe as a giant clockwork mechanism governed by physical laws</term>, grants things only the power of a machine, and claims that movement—indifferent in itself—produces diverse things only by reason of the various configurations of the bodies it moves, whereas the other, entirely intellectual, admits a universal soul of the World <term>universal soul: also known as the "Anima Mundi," this is the belief that a single, vital, and intelligent spirit permeates all of nature</term>, active, intelligent, and infor-* <note>The text breaks here at "infor-", likely continuing as "informante" (informing/giving form to) on the following page. A catchword "ma" is visible, suggesting the next page may begin with "matière" (matter).</note>

<summary>The translator compares Cartesian mechanical philosophy, which views the world as a machine, with Hermetic philosophy, which posits a living, universal soul animating all matter.</summary>
<keywords>René Descartes, Hermes Trismegistus, mechanical philosophy, universal soul, Anima Mundi, 17th-century science, alchemy</keywords>

# PREFACE.

## THE COMMENTATOR
### *to the Reader.*

There are so many books on <term>Chemistry: In this context, the term refers to Alchemy, the "Hermetic" art of transmuting metals and seeking spiritual perfection.</term>, whether printed or in manuscript, that one could say no science has ever had as many authors as that of Hermes <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure regarded as the father of alchemy and the "Hermetic" sciences.</note>. Happy father to have had such children, glorious Master to have had such disciples; you are rightly called the Master of Masters, each of your disciples being worthy of that name. But all these books are only for—

<summary>The commentator begins a grand tribute to Hermes Trismegistus, noting that while alchemy has more authors than any other science, they all descend from this single "Master of Masters."</summary>
<keywords>Hermes Trismegistus, alchemy, history of chemistry, Hermetic philosophy, master and disciple</keywords>

--- Page 12 ---
<meta>This page continues the Commentator's critique of alchemical literature, following the claim that many existing books are misleading or incomplete.</meta>

# PREFACE.

...not being true, not all having been produced by Authors who were truly such themselves; some are truncated, others altered; and what is worse, several are falsified; this stems only from the envy and rage of those who, through a lack of genius, or by a just punishment from God, were not able to be admitted to this table <note>The "table" likely refers to the *Tabula Smaragdina* (Emerald Tablet) or the metaphorical banquet of Hermetic wisdom shared by true initiates.</note>. It remains true, however, despite the depravity of the Age, that there are still good people whom Providence has reserved; not all have sucked this contagious venom, and there are some who have avoided the bite of the Serpent; especially those who have contemplated the bronze serpent <term>Bronze Serpent: A reference to the biblical Nehushtan (Numbers 21:9) which healed those who looked upon it; in alchemy, it often symbolizes the "fixing" of the volatile or the healing power of the philosopher's stone.</term> raised upon the mountain, who have entrusted their hopes to him, and have observed his holy Laws.

<summary>The author warns that many alchemical texts are corrupted or fraudulent due to the spite of those excluded from the craft's secrets, though a few "good people" remain guided by Providence.</summary>
<keywords>alchemical literature, forgery, Providence, Bronze Serpent, Hermetic laws, spiritual purity, Emerald Tablet</keywords>

--- Page 13 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's personal narrative following his warning about the corruption of alchemical texts. Having established the rarity of true wisdom, he begins to describe his own intellectual and spiritual journey into the study of Hermetic philosophy.</meta>

# PREFACE.

I had barely finished my **fifteenth year** <note>original: "troisiéme lustre". A *lustrum* is a Roman unit of time representing a five-year period; the author was fifteen years old at the start of his journey.</note>, when by I know not what instinct, I threw myself into the reading of these Books, and made every effort to gain an understanding of them: but my mind, finding itself blinded by the excessive brilliance of this **Light** <note>In this context, "Light" refers to the overwhelming or blinding nature of profound esoteric knowledge.</note>, and knowing that it was impossible for me to unravel the riddles of this **Sphinx** <term>Sphinx: A classical mythological figure known for posing riddles; alchemists frequently used the Sphinx to symbolize the obscure and difficult allegories of their craft.</term>, I left the Books there, I abandoned my reading, and renounced forever the hope of understanding them; however, after some time, having taken heart again, and implored **Divine aid** <note>original: "secours Divin". A common theme in alchemy is that the "Great Work" cannot be achieved through intellect alone, but requires the grace of God.</note>, full of a new hope, I set myself once more to reading day and night with all my strength, and consumed twelve years in this reading en-

<summary>The author recounts his early obsession with alchemical texts at age fifteen, his initial despair at their complexity, and his eventual return to intensive study through divine inspiration.</summary>
<keywords>autobiography, lustrum, illumination, Sphinx, riddles, divine grace, twelve years of study, alchemical pursuit</keywords>

-tire years <note>original: "entiéres". Completing the phrase from the previous page: "consumed twelve years in this reading entirely."</note>, after which I wanted to try if I could put into practice what I had conceived in my **mind**, but being uncertain, I would make one resolution, then another, and there always remained difficulties that I could not overcome; finally, I associated myself on two different occasions with two other people, and this **partnership** <note>original: "societé". While it translates literally as "society," in this context it refers to a small working group or collaborative study between aspiring alchemists.</note> gave me the opportunity to study better, because I was sometimes obliged to challenge their **opinions**, and sometimes also to approve them; but in truth we were all like blind men, and we took for a true **Light** <note>original: "Lumiere". In alchemical literature, "Light" represents the moment of true insight or the "Gift of God" that reveals the secrets of the work.</note> that which was only an effect of our desires and of some readings. Together we conducted several **experiments** <term>experiments: The French *experiences* at this time referred both to laboratory trials and to the knowledge gained through observation.</term>,

<summary>The author describes moving from solitary study to collaborative alchemical practice, admitting that he and his partners were "blind" and misled by their own enthusiasm rather than true understanding.</summary>

<meta>This page follows the Author's Preface, transitioning from the author's personal justification for the work to the official legal authorization required for its publication in 17th-century France.</meta>

## Extract of the King’s Privilege.

By the grace and Privilege <term>Privilege: a legal monopoly granted by the French Crown to a publisher, acting as an early form of copyright protection to ensure only one person could profit from a specific book.</term> of the King, given at Versailles on the 2nd day of May, 1686. Signed: LE PETIT. Permission is granted to LAURENT D’HOURY, Merchant Bookseller, to have printed a Book entitled *Light issuing by itself from Darkness, or the true Theory of the Philosophers’ Stone* <note>original: "La Lumiere sortant par soy même des Tenebres, ou la veritable Theorie de la Pierre des Philosophes." This title is characteristic of alchemical literature, which often used the metaphor of light emerging from shadows to describe the process of transmutation or spiritual enlightenment.</note>, in such volume, margin, and character, and as many times as he shall see fit, for the duration of six consecutive years. And all others are forbidden from printing it without the express consent of the Petitioner or his legal successors, under penalty of two thousand livres <note>A substantial fine for the 1680s, roughly equivalent to several years of income for a typical tradesman.</note> in fines, the confiscation of any counterfeit copies, and all costs, damages, and interests, as is more fully detailed in the said Privilege.

*Registered in the Book of the Guild of Printers and Booksellers of Paris on May 27, 1686. Signed: ANGOT.*

*Printing completed for the first time on November 15, 1686.*

## Extract of the King’s Privilege.

By the grace and Privilege <term>Privilege: a legal monopoly granted by the French Crown to a publisher, acting as an early form of copyright protection to ensure only one person could profit from a specific book.</term> of the King, given at Versailles on the 24th day of May 1686. Signed: LE TELLIER <note>Michel Le Tellier was a high-ranking French official; although he died in 1685, the name continued to represent the authority of the office of the Chancellor.</note>: permission is granted to LAURENT D’HOURY, Merchant Bookseller, to have printed a Book entitled *The Pyramid of itself by itself, etc. or the true Theory of the Philosophers’ Spirit* <note>original: "La Pyramide de soy par luy même, &c. ou la veritable Theorie de l'Esprit des Philosophes." This title refers to the "spirit" of the philosophers, likely a reference to the volatile essence or universal mercury sought in alchemy.</note>, in such volume, margin, and character, and as many times as he shall see fit, for the duration of ten consecutive years. And all others are forbidden from printing it without the express consent of the Petitioner or his legal successors, under penalty of two thousand livres <note>A significant fine for the era, intended to protect the publisher’s investment in the printing costs.</note> in fines, the confiscation of any counterfeit copies, and all costs, damages, and interests, as is more fully detailed in the said Privilege.

Registered in the Book of the Guild of Printers and Booksellers of Paris on May 24, 1686. Signed: ANGOT.

Printing completed for the first time on November 15, 1686.

<summary>This page presents the official legal authorization from the French Crown, granting Laurent d’Houry the exclusive ten-year right to publish a work on the "Philosophers' Spirit." It establishes the book’s legality and protected status against counterfeiters in the 17th-century Parisian book trade.</summary>
<keywords>Royal Privilege, Laurent d’Houry, alchemy, Philosophers' Spirit, copyright history, 17th-century printing, Versailles, Paris Guild of Printers</keywords>

### I

## SECOND CANTO <note>original: "Chant Deuxième." While "Chant" literally means song, in this context it refers to a major division or "Canto" of a philosophical or didactic work.</note>

### I.

How men little versed in the school of Hermes <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure regarded as the father of alchemy (the "Hermetic Art").</note> are mistaken when, with a spirit of greed, they cling to the sound of words. # The Light

## II.

But if they could open the eyes of their mind to truly understand the hidden meaning of the Authors, they would clearly see that the gold and quicksilver <note>original: "argent vif," literally "living silver," the common name for mercury.</note> of the common people <term>vulgar: from the Latin "vulgus," referring here to the uninitiated or those who take alchemical texts literally</term> are devoid of that universal fire <note>original: "feu universel." In alchemy, this is the vital life force or "Spirit of the World" believed to animate all matter.</note>, which is the true agent. This agent or spirit abandons the metals as soon as they are exposed to the violence of the furnace flames; this is why a metal, once removed from its mine and deprived of this spirit, is nothing more than a dead and immobile body.

## III.

It is quite another Mercury, and another gold, of which Hermes <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure to whom the foundational texts of alchemy are attributed.</note> spoke: a Mercury that is moist and hot, and always stable in the fire. A gold that is entirely fire and life. Is such a difference not enough to easily distinguish these from those of the common people, who 

<note>The vocabulary below highlights the key concepts discussed: universal fire, spirit, metals, Mercury, Hermes, gold, quicksilver, the common/uninitiated, and the dead body of metal.</note>

<summary>This page explains that common gold and mercury are "dead" because the smelting process drives out their vital "universal fire." It contrasts these common materials with the "Philosophical" gold and mercury, which are living, stable, and full of spirit.</summary>

# The Light

...they make dead bodies deprived of spirit, whereas ours are corporeal spirits, always living. <note>This completes the thought from the previous page: common smelting "kills" the metal by driving out its spirit, but the alchemical process preserves it.</note>

### IV.

O great Mercury of the Philosophers, it is in you that gold and silver unite, after they have been drawn from potential into action; <note>original: "puiſſance en acte." This is Aristotelian terminology: "potentiality" is the capacity to become something, while "actuality" is the realized state. The alchemist "activates" the latent power within the metal.</note> Mercury all Sun, and all Moon; <note>Sun and Moon are the traditional alchemical symbols for Gold and Silver, or the Masculine and Feminine principles.</note> triple substance in one, and one substance in three. O wonderful thing! Mercury, Sulfur, and Salt make me see three substances in a single substance. <term>Mercury, Sulfur, and Salt: Known as the "Tria Prima" or Three Primes, these were the fundamental components of all matter according to Paracelsian alchemy.</term>

### V.

But where then is this gold-making Mercury which, resolving into Salt and into Sulfur, becomes the radical moisture <note>original: "l'humide radical." This refers to the fundamental life-preserving fluid or "essential grease" believed to exist within all matter, which prevents it from being destroyed by heat.</note> of metals and their animated seed? It is imprisoned in a prison so strong that Nature herself would not know how to draw it out, if industrious art <note>In this context, "Art" refers to the skilled work of the alchemist, which completes what Nature has started.</note> did not facilitate the means for her.

### VI.

But what then does **art** <note>In this context, "Art" refers specifically to alchemy, often called the "Sacred Art" or the "Hermetic Art."</note> do? An ingenious minister of diligent **Nature**, it purifies with a vaporous flame the paths that lead to the prison. <note>The "prison" refers to the coarse, physical state of mineral matter that "shackles" the spiritual essence or "seed" inside.</note> There is no better guide nor surer means than that of a gentle and continuous heat to assist **Nature**, and give her the means to break the bonds with which our **Mercury** is shackled.

### VII.

Yes, yes, it is this **Mercury** alone that you must seek, O headstrong spirits, <note>original: "esprits indociles." The author addresses those who are difficult to teach or who look for the secret in the wrong places.</note> since in it alone can you find all that is necessary for the **Sages**. <term>Sages: A traditional term for accomplished alchemists (Philosophers) who have mastered the secrets of nature.</term> It is in this Mercury that both the **Moon** and the **Sun** <note>Moon and Sun are the standard alchemical symbols for Silver and Gold, representing the cooling/feminine and warming/masculine principles.</note> are found in proximate potential; <note>An Aristotelian concept: "proximate potential" implies the substance is already in a state very nearly ready to become its final form, requiring only the "Art" to trigger the change.</note> these, without the gold and silver of the common people, <note>original: "or & argent du vulgaire." Alchemists distinguished between "vulgar" metals (ordinary commercial gold and silver) and "philosophical" metals (the living essences extracted during the Work).</note> being united together, become the true seed of silver and gold.

### VIII.

But every seed is useless if it remains whole, if it does not rot and become black; for corruption <note>original: "corruption." ### IV.

It is with such a fire that the **Art** <term>Art: In this context, "Art" refers to Alchemy (the Hermetic Art), which was seen not as a rejection of nature, but as a method to accelerate and perfect natural processes.</term> which wishes to imitate **Nature** must work, and that the one must supply what the other lacks. Nature begins, Art finishes, and Art alone purifies what Nature could not purify. Art has **ingenuity** <note>original: "l'industrie"</note> as its share, and Nature has simplicity; so that if one does not smooth the path, the other stops immediately.

unknown to everyone, and yet everyone has it before their eyes; it is despised like mud by the ignorant masses, and is sold at a low price, but it is precious to the **Philosopher** <term>Philosopher: In this context, not a student of ethics or logic, but a "Philosopher by Fire"—an alchemist who understands the hidden laws of nature.</term> who knows its value.

### VI.

It is this matter, so despised by the ignorant, that the learned seek with care, since in it is everything they can desire: In it are found joined the **Sun** and the **Moon**, <note>In alchemy, the "Sun" (Sol) and "Moon" (Luna) represent the celestial influences of gold and silver, or more broadly, the active/masculine and passive/feminine principles of nature.</note> not the common ones, nor those that are dead. <note>original: "non les vulgaires, non ceux qui sont morts." Alchemists often distinguished between "vulgar" metals (common commercial gold and silver) and their "philosophical" counterparts, which were believed to be "alive" and capable of growth or transformation.</note> In it is enclosed the fire from which these metals draw their life; it is she <note>The "matter" is often personified as feminine in alchemical literature.</note> who gives the **fiery water**, <term>Fiery Water: Also known as *aqua ignita*, this refers to a powerful solvent or a substance that possesses both the fluidity of water and the transformative heat of fire.</term> and who also gives the **fixed earth**; <term>Fixed Earth: The stable, solid residue that remains after distillation or calcination, representing the physical "body" of the philosophical stone.</term> it is she, finally, who gives everything necessary to an enlightened mind.

### IX.

What purpose do all these various mixtures serve? Since our science encloses the entire **Mastery** <term>Mastery: From the Latin *magisterium*; the complete process and secret formula of the alchemical Great Work.</term> within a single root, which I have already made known to you sufficiently, and perhaps more than I should have. This root contains within it two substances which nonetheless have only a single essence; and these substances, which at first are only gold and silver in **potential**, finally become Gold and silver in **act**, <term>Potential vs. Act: Using Aristotelian philosophy, the author suggests that the raw material (potential) must be matured into its perfected form (act).</term> provided that we know how to properly equalize their weights.

---

### X.

Yes, these substances become Gold and Silver actually, and through the equality of their weights, the **volatile** <term>Volatile: A substance that easily evaporates or escapes the fire; "fixing" it means making it solid and resistant to heat.</term> is fixed into the **Sulfur of gold**. <note>original: "Souphre d'or." In alchemy, "Sulfur" refers to the active, masculine, and combustible principle of a substance, rather than the common yellow mineral.</note> O luminous Sulfur, O true **animated Gold**, I adore within you all the wonders and all the virtues of the **Sun**. <note>The Sun: The traditional alchemical symbol for Gold and the highest state of spiritual and physical perfection.</note> For your Sulfur

### PREFACE <note>original: "AVANT PROPOS"</note>

There are very few people who, hearing mention of the **Philosopher's Stone** <term>Philosopher's Stone: *la pierre Philosophale*, the legendary alchemical substance capable of turning base metals into gold and achieving physical and spiritual perfection.</term>, do not frown at the name alone and, by turning their heads away, reject this *Treatise*; But in truth, is it not a great injustice to thus blame what one does not know at all? Before passing judgment, one should at least know what they are condemning, and what the **Philosopher's Stone** actually is.

However, those who act in this way judge this science in relation to the **common artists** <term>common artists: *Artistes vulgaires*, a term used to describe "puffers" or fraudulent practitioners who pursued alchemy only for material wealth rather than philosophical truth.</term>, who instead of the **Stone** they promise to make, consume all their own wealth and that of others; and seeing so many impostures, so many false **recipes** <note>original: "receptes"</note>, and so many vain promises of **Charlatans**, they take occasion from this...

<summary>In this preface, the author defends alchemy against public prejudice, arguing that the science is unfairly judged by the failures and frauds of "common artists" and charlatans.</summary>
<keywords>Philosopher's Stone, alchemical defense, skepticism, common artists, charlatans, recipes, prejudice</keywords>

--- Page 59 ---
<meta>This page continues the rhetorical defense of alchemy from page 36. It shifts from criticizing charlatans to establishing the philosophical and naturalistic foundations of the "True Art," contrasting the failures of "philosophasters" with the deep natural knowledge required of a true adept.</meta>

->Foreword.<-

...to attack the truth of the **Art** <note>In this context, "Art" refers specifically to Alchemy, often called the "Sacred Art" or "Hermetic Art."</note>, not considering that this is not the work of ordinary **Chemists**, but of true **Philosophers**, and that it is just as difficult for these **philosophasters** <term>philosophasters: *Philosophatres*, a derogatory term for those who pretend to be philosophers or alchemists but lack true wisdom or scientific grounding.</term> to create this **Stone**, as it is to make the Moon descend to the Earth, or to produce a new Sun. To be a **Philosopher**, one must perfectly know the foundations of all **Nature**, for the science of the **Philosopher’s Stone** far surpasses all other sciences, and all other **Arts** however subtle they may be; there being always this difference between the works of **Nature**, and those of **Art**, that the former are the most perfect, the most complete, and the most certain; and if (following the **Axiom of Aristotle**) <note>original: "l'Axiome d'Aristote"</note> nothing is in the intellect which was not first in the senses <note>This is the famous peripatetic axiom: *Nihil est in intellectu quod non prius fuerit in sensu*. The author uses it to argue that alchemy is not a fantasy, but a science based on observing the physical world.</note>. It will be true to say, that what we conceive, we conceive only by the occasion of what **Nature** does every day before our eyes, for all the **Arts** have drawn their principles, and their first ideas from the...

<summary>The author argues that alchemy is a supreme science rooted in the laws of Nature, far exceeding the capabilities of "philosophasters" or fake alchemists. He invokes Aristotle's empiricism to suggest that the Art of alchemy is a refined imitation of natural processes observed by the senses.</summary>
<keywords>Nature vs. Art, Aristotle, Philosophasters, Alchemy as Science, Empiricism, Philosophical Stone, Chemists</keywords>

->Foreword.<-

...natural works, which is so well known to all those who possess an understanding beyond the common sort that it would be useless to attempt to justify it: But without dallying with vain discourse, one must know in general that the **Philosopher’s Stone** is nothing other than the **radical moisture** <term>radical moisture: *l'humide radical*, a vital fluid or "primordial sap" that alchemists and early physicians believed was the innate source of life, growth, and heat in all living things.</term> of the **Elements**, which is indeed spread throughout them, but reunited within their **Stone**, and stripped of all foreign impurity; thus, one must not be surprised if it can perform such great things, it being quite certain that the life of animals, plants, and minerals consists solely of their **radical moisture**; and just as a man who wished to keep a lamp burning would not fear that it might go out if he had a reserve of oil, because he would only need to replenish it as it was consumed; in the same way, when our **radical moisture**, in which the **fire of life** is enclosed, begins to be consumed, **Nature** needs to be supplied with new moisture through the...

<summary>The author defines the Philosopher's Stone as the concentrated "radical moisture" of the elements, the essential substance that fuels life. Using a lamp oil analogy, the text explains that the Stone acts as a restorative agent for the vital heat within living beings.</summary>

<keywords>Philosopher’s Stone, radical moisture, fire of life, elements, vitalism, alchemy, lamp analogy</keywords>

--- Page 61 ---
<meta>This page continues the analogy from page 37, where the author compares the human life force to a lamp. It explains how "radical moisture" acts as the fuel for "natural heat," and how alchemy (the Art) can provide a purified essence to restore vigor when ordinary nutrition fails.</meta>

->Foreword.<-

...means of nourishment, without which this light of life, freed from its bonds, would fly away. It happens, however, sometimes that the **natural heat** <term>natural heat: *chaleur naturelle*, the innate warmth believed to be the source of life and digestion in the body.</term> is so weakened in its **radical moisture** by some accident that it does not have the strength to take in more through **nutrition**, which makes it languish and causes it at last to abandon its body through death; but if one could give it an **essence** stripped of impurities <note>original: *excremens*, referring to waste matter or dross filtered out by a chemical or biological process.</note>, and perfectly purified by the **Art** <term>the Art: Alchemy, often called the "Great Art" or the "Spagyric Art."</term>; then without doubt, the **natural heat** would attract this **essence** to itself, convert it into its own nature, and restore to the body its former vigor; but all these medicines would be of no use to a dead man, however **balsamic** <term>balsamic: *balzamiques*, healing substances believed to have restorative and preservative properties similar to natural balms.</term> and perfect they might be, for only the **fire of nature** enclosed within the body appropriates the medicines to itself, and delivers itself by their means from the bad **humors** <term>humeurs: the fluids—blood, phlegm, yellow bile, and black bile—that early physicians believed governed health and temperament.</term> which prevent it from freely performing its **vital office**.

<summary>The author explains that while life depends on nourishment to maintain "natural heat," alchemy can provide a purified essence to restore a failing body. He emphasizes that medicine only works if the "fire of nature" is still present within the living person to process it.</summary>

<keywords>natural heat, radical moisture, nutrition, alchemy, balsamic, fire of nature, humors, vital office</keywords>

--- Page 62 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on the relationship between "natural heat" and "radical moisture" in the human body. The previous page established that the fire of life requires a purified fuel to restore vigor when the body is too weak to process ordinary food.</meta>

->Foreword.<-

within its own **radical moisture** <term>radical moisture: *humide radical*, the innate, vital fluid believed by early physicians to be the "oil" that fuels the lamp of life.</term>. It is therefore necessary, by way of **nutrition**, to provide it with a suitable and restorative food, and then this **vital fire** <term>vital fire: *feu vital*, the innate heat or spark of life that resides in the heart and drives the body's functions.</term> will recover its former strength; whereas other medicines do nothing but irritate nature, far from restoring it. Of what use would it be to a soldier mortally wounded, and who had lost all his blood, if one wanted to rouse him to combat by the sound of trumpets and the noise of drums, and pretended thereby to encourage him to endure the labors of Mars <note>original: *travaux de Mars*; a classical reference to the God of War, here representing the physical struggle of battle.</note>? Of no use, undoubtedly; on the contrary, it would only harm him and do nothing but instill in him a fatal terror. It is the same with a nature debilitated and languishing through the loss or suffocation of its **radical moisture**; and nothing would be so dangerous or so useless as to irritate it with medicines; but if one could increase and strengthen the **radical moisture**, then nature would of itself rid itself of its waste <note>original: *excremens*, meaning metabolic waste products or impurities within the humors.</note> and its superfluities.

<summary>The author argues that a weakened body cannot be forced into health by harsh medicines, comparing it to a dying soldier who cannot be revived by the sounds of war. Instead, one must replenish the "radical moisture" to allow nature to heal itself and expel impurities.</summary>

->Foreword.<-

One can say the same thing regarding the vegetable and the mineral <note>This refers to the three kingdoms of nature—animal, vegetable, and mineral—suggesting that the same laws of vital essence apply to plants and stones as they do to humans.</note>. One can only be astonished at the stubbornness of those who are constantly occupied with remedies for health, and yet remain entirely ignorant of the source from which both health and life flow. Let these people no longer presume to speak of the **Philosopher's Stone** <term>Philosopher's Stone: *Pierre Philosophale*, the legendary alchemical substance capable of turning base metals into gold and acting as an elixir of life.</term>, since they use their reason so poorly.

In conclusion, I say that he to whom God has freely granted the possession of this **Stone**, and given the spirit to use it well, will not only enjoy perfect health, but will also be able, with the help of **Providence**, to prolong his days beyond the ordinary term, and have the means to praise God in a long and sweet life.

It is an inviolable law of **Nature**, that whenever a body is attacked by a disease resulting from the conflict of qualities <term>conflict of qualities: *contrariété des qualitez*, referring to the imbalance between the four primary qualities (hot, cold, moist, and dry) which was believed to cause illness.</term>, it falls into ruin, because

### Foreword.

—benign and fertile, a **natural heat** <term>natural heat: *chaleur naturelle*, the innate vital warmth that early modern medicine believed was the primary engine of life, digestion, and healing.</term> in which it abounds. It is through this that it works admirable and incredible cures in the bodies of living beings, when, instead of employing the hand of the **Physician**, nature alone serves at once as Physician and remedy.

All ordinary medicines do nothing, as we have said, but irritate nature and force it to gather all its strength against them; from which it happens that after having taken some remedy, one remains for a long time languid and dejected. **Nature** alone knows how to expel **waste matter** <note>original: "excremens"; in this context, it refers to the "dross" or metabolic byproducts that the body needs to purge to restore balance.</note>, and it is this single faculty which is necessary on such an occasion; for to give **purgatives** to a weakened body is only to aggravate the illness and increase the waste instead of diminishing it; but since it is the property of nature, when a man is in health, to expel **superfluous humors** <term>superfluous humors: *humeurs superfluës*, an excess of the body's four vital fluids (blood, phlegm, yellow bile, and black bile) which were thought to cause disease if they became unbalanced.</term> of its own accord, why, when nature is languid, should we not try to strengthen it and communicate to it...

Only the mercurial, humid substance of the metal can be changed in this way; those who claim otherwise are impostors, for no alteration can occur except between similar natures. ->#### Foreword.<-

...menstruum <note>original: "menstruë"; an alchemical term for a powerful solvent or "universal" liquid agent used to dissolve or "open" metallic bodies.</note> were transmuted into **Gold**, they tell us a lie, and they do not understand the nature of metals; for even though one part appears to be **Gold**, and the other retains its original metallic form, it does not follow from this that a transmutation has occurred. Instead, it is an **imposture**, and is nothing more than a piece of natural **Gold** skillfully joined to another piece of imperfect metal—in truth, with such precision that it indeed seems to be a whole nail <note>The transcription shows "cloud," but in the context of alchemical history and the preceding sentence, this is "clou" (nail). A common trick involved soldering a gold tip to an iron nail and painting it to look uniform.</note>, but the fraud is easily discovered by an enlightened mind.

These were the things by which I remained persuaded of the truth of the **science** <term>science: here referring specifically to the "Hermetic Science" or Alchemy, which practitioners viewed as a rigorous study of natural laws.</term>, and I believe they will suffice for any man of good understanding, provided he always relates them to the possibility of **Nature**; nevertheless, he may still consult other Authors; but before...

### The Light

there can be neither duality nor any division whatsoever; what is also more indivisible, what greater unity is there than the Divine Word? <term>Divine Word: Translating the Latin "Verbum" or French "Verbe," referring to the "Logos"—the creative speech or intellect of God through which the universe was made.</term> The point of the center, no less indivisible than invisible, can only be understood through the circumference; in the same way, the invisible Word of God is only comprehensible through his creatures; <note>In this historical context, "creatures" refers to all created things in the universe, including inanimate objects and celestial bodies, not just living beings.</note> all lines are drawn from the center and terminate at the center; similarly, everything that is created has come forth from the Word of God, and will return to Him after the circular revolution of time. The point of the center remains motionless while the wheel turns; likewise, the Word of God remains immutable while all other things are subject to changes and vicissitudes. As all things have emanated from the center by extension, so all things will return to the center by contraction; <note>Original: "resserrement," implying a gathering back or inward drawing, the mathematical and physical opposite of the initial expansion.</note> the one was performed by an uncreated goodness, the other will be performed by an impenetrable wisdom.

# The Light

<page-num>84</page-num>

...being of itself nothing other than the division of the waters, and a certain expanse in which the **Light** was to be spread to enlighten and <term>inform<note>In the philosophical sense of the time, "to inform" meant to give form, essence, or life to matter, rather than just providing information.</note></term> the world; but as **Light** is of a spiritual nature, and consequently invisible, it was necessary to clothe it in some opaque body, by means of which it could be perceptible to other creatures. This obliged the sovereign **Creator** to form **luminaries** from the mass of the **superior waters**, from which He made various bodies according to His will, and distributed to them the **Light** necessary to shine here and there. And as in all the bodies of this lower region, the **inferior waters** served to provide the matter which was needed, it must also be said that all the **celestial bodies** were formed only from the single matter of the **superior waters**; for what use indeed would there be in multiplying matters, since from a single **chaos** all the various distinctions that have been made could be produced.

## CHAPTER III.

Only the children of the <term>hermetic science<note>Alchemy and the study of the secret laws of nature, named after the mythical figure Hermes Trismegistus.</note></term> know the true foundations of all Nature, and they alone, enlightened by this beautiful Light, deserve the name of <term>Physicists<note>original: "Phificiens." ### V.

*If I have understood correctly,* <note>The author provides a poetic summary of the alchemical process, translated here from the original Italian verse.</note>
*That your **unknown Mercury** is nothing else* <note>original: *Mercurio ignoto*. In alchemy, "Mercury" is not just the liquid metal, but the universal principle of fluidity and transformation.</note>
*Than a living, innate **universal Spirit**.*
*Which descends from the Sun*
*In aerial vapor ever in motion*
*To fill the empty center of the Earth:*
*From which it then emerges*
*Amidst **impure Sulphurs**,* <note>original: *Solfi impuri*. Sulphur represents the principle of combustion and structure, which at this stage is still "unrefined" or "impure."</note> *and grows*
*From volatile to fixed, and having taken form,*
*It shapes itself into **radical moisture**.* <term>Radical moisture: Also known as *humidum radicale*, this was believed to be the fundamental life-sustaining fluid found within all matter, acting as the "root" of its existence.</term>

This double movement, and this double nature of Mercury, mean that it is considered under two different aspects; for before its congelation <note>The process of thickening or becoming solid; in alchemy, the transition from a spiritual/vaporous state to a physical one.</note> and in the path of descent, it is the aerial and most pure vapor of the Elements, of the nature of the superior waters <note>Celestial or divine waters, often contrasted with the "inferior waters" of the material Earth.</note>, carrying naturally in its breast the spirit of Light, and the true fire of Nature. It is the wind or the air of the Heavens which carries in its belly the fertility of the Sun, and which with its wings covers the nakedness of the fire <note>This is a poetic reference to the *Emerald Tablet of Hermes Trismegistus*, a foundational alchemical text which states: "The wind carried it in its belly."</note>.

## CHAPTER VII.

**AFTER** our Author has made us, as if touching it with a finger <note>original: "toucher au doigt"; a French idiom meaning to see or understand something with absolute clarity or through direct experience.</note>, grasp our **divine science** <note>divine science: a standard historical term for alchemy, which practitioners viewed as a sacred gift from God rather than merely a physical craft.</note>, he excuses himself for not saying more about it, because there remains much for him to learn himself, and he confesses that he should have

...show more knowledge, for having to speak to learned people, he even fears that something might be missing from his work, and that the proper order might not be well-maintained. Learn from this, you **sellers of smoke** <note>original: "vendeurs de fumée"; a derogatory historical term for charlatans or "puffers"—false alchemists who deceived people with impressive-looking but worthless chemical displays.</note>, how difficult it is to perform **our Work** <note>our Work: referring to the "Great Work" (Magnum Opus) of alchemy.</note>, since it is not a matter of performing common operations, which although perfect in their own kind, are useless to our purpose and despised by all the **Philosophers** <note>Philosophers: in alchemical literature, this term refers specifically to true adepts and practitioners of "Natural Philosophy," not modern academic philosophers.</note>. There is only one operation, as we have said in our **Magisterium** <term>Magisterium: The "Grand Magisterium" refers to the successful completion of the alchemical process or the "Mastery" required to produce the Philosopher's Stone.</term>, and all the Philosophers teach it to us, warning us to abandon all these **sophistic operations** <note>sophistic operations: fraudulent or deceptive chemical processes that mimic the appearance of transmutation without the true physical and spiritual transformation.</note> and to hold fast to nature alone, where the truth resides.

It is within **Philosophical sublimation** <term>Philosophical sublimation: Distinct from common chemical sublimation (turning a solid into vapor), this is a "subtle" process of purifying the matter and raising it to a higher, more spiritualized state.</term> that all other operations are contained, and in it alone consists everything the **artist** <note>artist: a standard term for the alchemical practitioner or "operator."</note> can do that is best and most subtle; if, therefore, someone knows how to perform it well, he may boast of having known one of the greatest...

<meta>This page completes the sentence from page 149, which described the failures of misguided alchemists who seek a "Very Fixed Stone" but end up with nothing. It then transitions into a critique of the secrecy and "envy" of alchemical authors who intentionally mislead their readers.</meta>

<page-num>150</page-num>
<header>The Light</header>

...nothing other than an <term>Aerial Stone</term> <note>original: "Pierre aërienne"; a substance mistakenly thought to be captured from the atmosphere or morning dew.</note> and the **Sulphur of fools**.

The infinite errors of those who work <note>Meaning those practicing "the work" (the Great Work of alchemy) in their laboratories.</note> stem only from the fact that the **Philosophers** <note>The term "Philosophers" here refers to established alchemical masters and authors of hermetic texts.</note> deliberately deceive those who read them, imagining that by this means they will turn them away from the work. But they deceive themselves; for everyone loves their own error so much that they set to work again with more heat and confidence than they did before. 

The cause of so many misfortunes is thus solely the **envy** <note>In alchemical literature, "envy" refers to the tradition of "Hermetic Secrecy," where authors intentionally obscured their recipes to prevent the "unworthy" from succeeding.</note> of the Authors. This is why our **Poet**, terrified by so many kinds of errors into which those who attach themselves to this **science** fall, doubts himself and his own work, humbly imploring the indulgence of the **Philosophers**, and especially of those who, not being infected by the venom of envy, practice all its duties and are clothed in a truly **Philosophical charity**. It is of these men that one could never speak too much nor too well, for they are the **oracles of Nature**.

# La Lumière

...theless, if someone, in order to multiply it, put it in the ground, it would take a great deal of time, trouble, and care to lead it as far as **vegetation** <note>In alchemy, 'vegetation' refers to the process where metals are made to 'grow' and multiply within a vessel, mimicking the life cycle of plants.</note>; but if, instead of that, one took a graft, or a ~~one~~ root of the same tree, and put it in the ground, one would see it in a short time and without trouble vegetate and yield much fruit. It is the same with **Gold**; it is the fruit of the **Mineral Earth** and the **Solar Tree** <note>The 'Solar Tree' is a traditional alchemical metaphor for the origin of Gold, which was believed to be governed by the Sun.</note>, but a fruit of a very solid **mixture** <term>mixtion</term> <note>Original: *mixtion*. This refers to the specific proportion and union of the four elements—earth, air, fire, and water—that constitute a substance.</note>, and the most finished compound of **Nature**, which—because of this equality of **Elements** found within it—suffers with great difficulty the corruption and alteration of its qualities in order to pass into a new generation. It is, therefore, a very difficult and almost impossible undertaking to claim to put it into the **Earth** to **reincrudate** <term>reincrudate</term> <note>To 'reincrudate' (from the Latin *rudis*, meaning raw) is to return a perfected metal to its raw, 'crude,' or liquid-like state so it can be transformed again.</note> it and lead it to vegetation; but if instead of that one takes its root or its graft, one will much more easily have that...

# The Light

...because of which it has acquired malleability under the hammer, stability in fire, and a **citrine** <note>original: "citrine"; a yellowish-orange color characteristic of gold.</note> color; but this does not mean that **Quicksilver** <term>argent vif</term> <note>Common, liquid mercury.</note> has the power to dissolve it, nor that it could ever acquire it, especially since it [the gold] has passed into another substance, and has lost its original purity and simplicity, having become a metallic body very abundant in **superfluous humidity** <note>In alchemical theory, an excess of "watery" or "mercurial" moisture that has not been properly refined or "fixed" by heat.</note>, and burdened by an **earthy lividness** <note>A dull, dark, or leaden quality associated with the impurities of the earth.</note> which renders it incapable of this action.

It would be a great foolishness to imagine that by putting the **seed** <term>semence</term> of one man with the blood of another man, one could achieve a new generation, based on the reasoning that the **seed** is nothing other than the most pure part of the blood, which has undergone a great **digestion** <note>The process of maturation or "cooking" by internal heat, whether in a body or a vessel.</note>, and that blood is only more humid and raw; but if instead of that, the **sperm** were cast into the **matrix** <term>matrice</term> <note>The womb.</note> of a woman where there is a menstrual blood—

—menstrual blood, very raw, which through the virtue of the **Salt of the womb** <term>Sel de la matrice</term> <note>In alchemical theory, 'Salt' is the third principle that stabilizes the spirit and soul; here it refers to the specific reactive environment of the 'vessel'.</note> has acquired a certain sharpness and **astringency** <term>ponticité</term> <note>A quality of being sour or pungent, thought to be necessary for the 'biting' or dissolving action of a solvent.</note>, then this sperm, finding itself in its own vessel, would undoubtedly **return to a raw state** <term>reincruderoit</term> <note>To 're-incrudate' is to take a substance that has been matured or 'cooked' and return it to its primal, liquid, or 'raw' state so it can be transformed again.</note> by way of **putrefaction** <note>The stage where the matter turns black and decomposes, seen as essential for any new life or creation to begin.</note>, and would pass on to a new generation. It is the same with **Quicksilver** <term>Argent vif</term>, for although it is of the same nature as Gold and, through its abundant humidity, it easily insinuates itself into Gold’s pores and causes a disintegration of its smallest parts so that it appears dissolved; nevertheless, it would be a great error to believe such a dissolution to be good. This is properly nothing other than a **corrosion** of the metal, similar to those performed with common **aqua fortis** <term>eaux fortes</term> <note>Nitric acid or other strong mineral acids used by goldsmiths to separate metals.</note>. Such Quicksilver is not our **menstrual blood** <term>sang menstruel</term> <note>A metaphorical term used by alchemists to describe their 'Secret Mercury' or solvent, which acts as the nourishing environment for the 'seed' of gold.</note>, and it is only to deceive the ignorant that Authors use this ambiguous name.

# Light

[...substan]ces are not at all suitable for the **Physical Work** <term>Physical Work</term> <note>The actual lab-based transmutation process of the Sages, as opposed to theoretical or common chemistry.</note>, not only regarding their own substance, but also because they lack one thing which in our **Art** <term>Art</term> <note>Alchemists often referred to their practice simply as 'The Art', meaning the mastery of nature's hidden laws.</note> is an absolute necessity, namely a proper agent. I do not mean to speak here of that internal agent which is the virtue of the **Solar Sulfur** <term>Solar Sulfur</term> <note>The active, masculine, and "fiery" principle found within gold, believed to be the seed of the Great Work.</note>, of which we will speak later; but of the external agent which must excite the internal, and lead it from **potentiality into act** <note>A concept from Aristotelian philosophy; the external agent acts as a catalyst to turn a hidden "power" (potential) into a real, physical result (actuality).</note>; now this agent has been separated from the Gold at the end of its **decoction** <term>decoction</term> <note>The process of prolonged heating or 'cooking' used to mature the alchemical matter.</note>, that is to say, as a new form of Gold was introduced into the matter, this agent withdrew after having, however, imprinted its own virtue upon it (as the author of the book titled *The Precious Pearl* <note>original: "Margarita pretiosa"; a 14th-century alchemical treatise by Petrus Bonus of Ferrara.</note> very well argues regarding this matter) so that there remained only a single material substance determined by the action of the internal agent after its excitation. [separa]ted from **Gold** this agent because they cannot coexist; why would we wish to rejoin it once more? ### II.

## CHAPTER II.

Our Poet seems to subscribe to the opinion that we have just explained, by stating that common metals <term>metaux vulgaires: ordinary, "dead" metals like lead or copper used in trade, which alchemists believed were stripped of their creative power.</term> are without spirit or agent, because they have lost it in the melting <note>original: "fusion"; the process of extracting metal from ore using intense heat.</note>; which suggests that all metals, while still being in their mines, have with...

<summary>This page utilizes poetic verse to argue that commercially smelted metals are "dead" because the furnace's heat has driven out their vital "acting spirit." It establishes that only metals in their natural, unrefined state within the mine retain the "universal fire" necessary for alchemical transformation.</summary>

But regarding the generation of metals, we say that if this double vapor reaches a place where the fatness of Sulfur <term>fatness of Sulfur: a term used by alchemists to describe the oily, combustible, and sticky quality of the Sulfur principle</term> is adherent, they unite together and form a certain glutinous substance that resembles a shapeless mass. From this mass—by the action of Sulfur acting upon the vaporous moisture which is abundant in those places—a pure or impure metal is formed, depending on the purity or impurity of the locations. For if these vapors are pure and the locations are also very pure, a very pure metal will be engendered, namely Gold; from which its own agent <note>agent: the active force or "Sulfur" that drives the transformation</note> will be separated at the end of the decoction <term>decoction: a process of "cooking" or refining by natural heat within the earth over long periods</term>, so that nothing remains but the mercurial moisture <note>original: "humidité mercurielle"; the passive, fluid principle of matter</note> alone, but coagulated. And if it happens that the decoction is not completed, and the Sulfur is not entirely separated, then various imperfect metals <note>imperfect metals: in alchemy, any metal other than gold or silver, such as lead, tin, or copper, which were seen as "diseased" or unfinished versions of gold.</note> will be engendered, which will be more or less so in pro-

easy to see how far <term>Vitriol</term> <note>A sulfate mineral, often called "green vitriol." While central to many alchemical recipes, the author here argues it is too far removed from the "living" state of metal to be useful.</note> is from the generation of metals, and how they delude themselves who work upon it as if it were the true matter of the <term>Stone</term> <note>The Philosopher's Stone</note>, in which the true metallic essence must actually reside.

One also sees that metals, while they are in their mines, possess their own active agent; however, they are deprived of it by fusion <note>the process of smelting or melting ore to extract metal</note>, and retain only the outer bark and the envelope of this <term>Sulfur</term> <note>original: "Souphre"; in alchemical theory, Sulfur represents the active, masculine, and combustible principle that gives metal its form.</note> which is properly the dross of the metal. *Which, dissolved into sulfur and salt,*
*Becomes the radical moisture*
*Of metals—the animated seed?*

*In a dungeon so harsh,*
*That even Nature herself*
*Cannot extract him from that rugged prison,*
*Unless Masterful Art opens the way.*

## CHAPTER V.

The <term>Sulfur of the Philosophers</term> <note>Not common sulfur, but the active, masculine principle that gives form and "fire" to matter.</note> is, as we have said, enclosed within the innermost part of the <term>radical moisture</term> <note>The essential, life-sustaining fluid thought to exist within all matter; in alchemy, this is the "mercury" that must be purified.</note>, but imprisoned under such a hard crust that it can only rise into the air through the extreme industry of Art; for Nature does not possess in the mines a suitable <term>menstruum</term> <note>A solvent or liquid substance capable of dissolving solids, named for the "cycles" of time often required for the dissolution process.</note> capable of dissolving and liberating this Sulfur.

spirits remain constantly within bodies until they are entirely consumed and reduced to nothing; that is to say, as long as the <term>radical moisture: In alchemy and early medicine, the essential, life-sustaining fluid inherent in all matter, acting as the fuel for the "lamp of life."</term> subsists in whole or in part. But once this moisture is destroyed, the **specific virtue** is also destroyed. This <term>innate heat: The vital internal warmth that animates a living body or substance.</term>, enriched by its **specific spirit**, resides—as we have said—within the royal domain of the **radical moisture**, like the **Sun** in its own **Sphere**. <page-num>244</page-num>

...for if the grain remained in the earth without being <term>reincrudated</term> <note>returned to a raw, "crude" state to unlock its potential</note> and reduced into this <term>first matter</term>, the Laborer <note>original: "le Laboureur"—a metaphor for the alchemist</note> would wait in vain for the desired harvest; all seeds are useless for <term>multiplication</term> <note>the process of increasing the quantity and power of the alchemical stone</note> if they are not first **reincrudated**. This is why it is very necessary to perfectly understand this **reincrudation**, or reduction into **first matter**, by which alone this second <term>solution</term> <note>the dissolution of a solid substance into a liquid</note> of the **Physical body** can be achieved.

Regarding the third **solution**, it is properly that moistening of the earth or **Physical and mineral Sulfur**, through which the child <note>a reference to the burgeoning Philosopher's Stone</note> increases its strength; but since it relates primarily to **multiplication**, we shall refer the Reader to what the Authors have written on it.

This is what we had to say briefly on the subject of **solution**, so that the Reader may well understand all that belongs to the **theory**, and that with this help, they may read the Writings of the **Philosophers** <note>the term alchemists used to describe themselves</note> more boldly, and more easily disentangle themselves from their snares <note>a reference to the "Philosophical snares" or riddles intentionally placed in texts to mislead the unworthy</note>.

<summary>The author explains that substances must be returned to their "first matter" (reincrudation) to be productive, concluding the theoretical section on the three types of alchemical solutions.</summary>
<keywords>reincrudation, first matter, multiplication, physical body, sulfur, philosophical snares, alchemical theory</keywords>

### II.

> Cease now from your insane labors, <note>original Italian: "Da l'infane fatiche homai cessate"</note>
> Nor let blind hope any longer,

# CHAPTER II.

In this Chapter, following the order of our Poet, we ought to speak of the ridiculous work of **ignorant Artists** <note>original: "Artistes ignorans"—often called "puffers," these were practitioners who focused solely on chemical experiments without understanding the underlying philosophy.</note>; but because we have already said something of it here and there, and because we shall have further occasion to speak of it, we will not dwell upon it for the present, for fear of being too prolix <note>excessively wordy</note>, we will content ourselves only with warning the Reader on the subject of the **fire**, that one must not understand it as a

<summary>The author uses an Italian poem to warn against the physical toll and futility of "ignorant" alchemy, then begins a discussion on the true nature of alchemical fire.</summary>
<keywords>Hermetic Stone, Sages, ignorant artists, alchemical fire, poetic warning, puffers</keywords>

<header>The Light</header>
<sig>X iii</sig>

fire of charcoal, of manure, <note>Manure was frequently used in early chemistry and alchemy to provide a gentle, constant heat through the natural process of fermentation.</note> of lamp, nor of any other kind whatever; but that it is the fire used by **Nature**, this fire so well hidden among the **Philosophers**, <note>In this context, "Philosophers" refers to the true masters of alchemy, or "adepts."</note> and of which they speak only very obscurely; the construction of which is as difficult as it is secret, and if the **Artists** <note>original: "Artistes"—referring to those who practice the laboratory arts.</note> knew it, we could boldly assure that they would only need to undertake the **work of the Philosophers** <note>original: "l’œuvre des Philosophes"—the *Magnum Opus*, or the Great Work of creating the Philosopher's Stone.</note> to succeed in it; but so that the Reader may be convinced of our good intentions on this subject, we shall proceed to the explanation of the Chapter which follows.

## CHAPTER III.

I AM not surprised if many, and indeed almost all, have gone astray for lack of knowing the fire; for it is as if someone lacked the instruments necessary for their **Art**; <note>In alchemical texts, "the Art" specifically refers to the practice of alchemy itself.</note> it is certain that they would never reach the goal they set for themselves, and would produce nothing but that which is crippled and imperfect.

Therefore, so that your works may exist, O **children of the Art**, <note>A traditional term for alchemists, who viewed their work as a sacred craft or "Art."</note> make use of this **instrumental fire** <term>instrumental fire: also known as "philosophical fire," this is the specific energy or agent used by the alchemist to catalyze natural processes.</term> by which alone all things are made perfect. This fire is spread throughout all of **Nature**, for without it she would not know how to act; and wherever the **vegetative virtue** <note>The inherent power within living things—plants, minerals, and animals—that allows them to grow and maintain life.</note> is preserved, there also this fire is hidden.

This fire is always found joined to the **radical moisture** <note>original: "l’humide radical." In alchemy, this is the essential, life-sustaining fluid found in the heart of all matter; it was believed that the loss of this moisture led to aging and death.</note> of things, and continually accompanies the **crude seed** <note>original: "sperme cru." This refers to the raw, unrefined "seed" or potential within minerals and metals before they are matured by nature.</note> of bodies; but although it is thus spread throughout all of lower Nature and dispersed within the **Elements**, it nevertheless remains unknown to the world, and its actions are not sufficiently considered.

It is this fire that causes the corruption of things, for it is a very crude spirit, an enemy of rest, which demands nothing but war and destruction. It is a thing that one cannot admire too much in Nature, that everything which is found exposed to the air, everything which is in the water, or under the earth, reduces itself to nothing, and returns into its **first**... <note>The text cuts off here, continuing the word "premier" (first/original) onto the next page.</note>

...first **chaos** <note>original: "premier cahos." The primordial, unformed state of matter from which all things are born and to which they return.</note>. The most solid stones, the strongest towers, the most superb buildings, the hardest marbles, and finally all metals except gold, are reduced to powder after a long passage of time. The **ignorant common people** <note>original: "Le vulgaire ignorant." In alchemical texts, this refers to those who lack the secret knowledge of the "Art" and only see the surface of natural phenomena.</note> are accustomed to attributing such a surprising thing to time, which devours everything; and this comes from the fact that they are unaware of what is hidden within the **Elements**, and above all within the air.

It is an **invisible and imperceptible flame**, which imperceptibly consumes everything and enshrouds it in a profound silence. This fire of which we speak is diffused in the air because it is entirely aerial by its Nature; through its **crude spirit** it decomposes **mixtures** <term>mixtures: "les mixtes," a technical term for any substance formed by the combination of the four elements (Earth, Air, Fire, Water).</term>, and by destroying the works of Nature, it reduces all things to their **first being** <note>The essential, original state of a substance before it was formed into a specific object.</note> by means of **corruption** <term>corruption: in alchemy, this refers to the chemical breakdown or "death" of a substance, which is a necessary step before it can be "reborn" or purified.</term>; it is by this fire that the lead coverings of certain buildings are, after a long time, converted into a white rust that resembles **artificial white lead** <term>artificial white lead: "ceruse," a white pigment traditionally produced by exposing lead to vinegar vapors; the author argues that the air performs this same alchemical process naturally.</term>,

It has drawn from **Sulphur** <note>original: "Souphre." This usually refers to the external heat of the furnace or to corrosive substances (like strong acids) that "attack" and break down the chemical compounds.</note> is that which corrupts the compound, and which is the first to have the power to dissolve what Nature had strongly bound together; it is veiled under an infinity of names so as to be better hidden from the ignorant, and to know it well, one must study much, read and reread the **Authors**, and always compare what they say with the possibilities of Nature. Besides these, there are various [other] fires, such as those of dung, the water-bath <note>The *balneum Mariae* or "bain-marie," used for gentle, even heating.</note>, ashes, tree bark, nut shells, and oil...

### V.

> So to what purpose are so many substances,
> and so many
> In **retorts**, in **alembics**,
> If the matter is one, and the fire is one?
> Unique is the Matter, and in every place
> The Poor and the Rich possess it;
> It is unknown to all, yet before everyone’s eyes.
> Abject to the ignorant masses,
> Who always sell it like mud at a base price,
> But precious to the philosopher, who understands.
<note>original Italian: "Dunque à che prò tante ſoſtanze... Pretioſa al filoſofo, che intende." This verse summarizes a core hermetic paradox: the "Prime Matter" required for the Great Work is not a rare or expensive ingredient, but a common substance overlooked by the uninitiated.</note>

<meta>This page continues the discussion from the previous page regarding the "fixed earth" and how Nature acts as a guide in the alchemical process. just as a prudent mother has given you, my dear Reader, in this single subject everything you can desire, so that you may extract the kernel from it and prepare it for your use.

This earth, by its fiery and innate dryness, attracts its own moisture to itself and consumes it; and for this reason, it is compared to the **Dragon that devours its tail** <note>This refers to the *Ouroboros*, a symbol of the alchemical work being a circular process where the matter is purified by its own internal spirit, dissolving and congealing in a self-contained system.</note>. Furthermore, it only attracts and assimilates its own moisture because it is of its very same nature. 

By this is revealed the folly of those who vainly attempt to unite and congeal—by means of their various waters—things that are entirely opposite and as far apart from one another as Heaven is from the Earth, and between which there is not the slightest attraction. External heat is not capable of congealing water, no matter the degree to which that heat is applied; far from it, such heat dissolves the water and rarefies it by raising it into the air. But the **internal heat** of our **Physical earth** <term>Physical earth<gloss>In alchemical texts, "Physical" or "Philosophical" earth refers to the solid, fixed principle—the "Salt"—found within the primary matter, as opposed to common dirt or soil.</gloss></term> operates

It warns against trying to combine incompatible substances and distinguishes between destructive external heat and the transformative internal heat of the matter.</summary>

### Emerging from the Shadows.

Glass being thus a very pure portion of earth and water deprived of its **radical moisture** <term>radical moisture</term> <note>Known in Latin as *humidum radicale*, this was thought to be the essential fluid that acted as a "glue" for matter, providing flexibility and life; without it, substances become brittle or inert.</note>, as we have shown in the Chapter on **Mercury** <term>Mercury</term> <note>In this context, Mercury refers to the alchemical principle of volatility and the "soul" of metals, rather than just the common liquid metal.</note>, it would not be surprising that by restoring to it a new **radical moisture**, its parts would bind together and form a certain homogeneous being. Finally, an infinity of miracles can be performed by this path, which will nevertheless be nothing more than the effect of simple **natural magic** <term>natural magic</term> <note>Natural magic was a respected branch of Renaissance and early modern science that sought to understand and utilize the hidden powers of the natural world, such as magnetism or chemical reactions, without resorting to the supernatural.</note>, but which the ignorant will believe to be the works of the devil, failing to reflect that it is a sacrilege and an impiety to attribute to this evil spirit that which is due to **Nature** <term>Nature</term> alone, or to the **Author of Nature** <term>Author of Nature</term> <note>A common phrasing used by alchemists to refer to God as the ultimate source of natural laws.</note>.

In place of an epilogue, we merely advise the Reader that if he reads these things in the spirit of a wise curiosity, and with the desire to be instructed, we are very willing to joyfully dedicate this Writing to his leisure, so that he may gather the fruit he desires, in proportion to the breadth and capacity of his mind, this

# The Light

...which we pray God to grant him. Whoever, therefore, approaches without this true disposition shall return without any fruit.

### In the Foreword.

The Author provides a defense of the **Philosopher's Stone** <term>Philosopher's Stone</term> <note>The legendary substance capable of turning base metals into gold and providing the Elixir of Life.</note>, and defines it as nothing other than the **radical moisture** <term>radical moisture</term> <note>original: "l'humide radical"; a central alchemical concept referring to the fundamental, life-giving fluid or "sap" inherent in all matter that sustains its existence.</note> of the **Elements** <term>Elements</term> <note>Referring to the four classical elements: Earth, Air, Fire, and Water.</note> perfectly purified, and brought to a **Sovereign fixity** <term>fixity</term> <note>original: "fixité"; the quality of a substance that remains stable and does not evaporate or change when exposed to intense heat.</note>; this is why it performs such great things for health, as life resides solely in the radical moisture.

He demonstrates the excellence of the **Universal Medicine** <term>Universal Medicine</term> <note>Also known as the Panacea; a single remedy believed to cure all diseases by restoring the body's internal balance.</note>, and the advantage it holds over specific remedies, criticizing those who cling to the streams of this fountain instead of taking it from its source.

# Summary

...ment, which separates the waters from the waters. He gives the reason why the **rarefaction** <term>rarefaction: the process by which a substance becomes less dense, often through heating or spiritual refinement, causing it to rise</term> of the waters ends at the **first heaven**, and why they do not rise beyond it, since the nature of rarefied things is to always rise upward; and he claims that this is due to nothing other than God, in His eternal laws, having assigned to each thing its own proper **sphere**.

He mocks **judicial astrology** <note>Judicial astrology: the practice of predicting specific human fortunes and earthly events based on the stars, often criticized by theologians for infringing on free will or divine providence.</note>.

He says that the **superior waters** served as the matter for **celestial bodies**, just as the **inferior waters** serve as the matter for the bodies here below.

<meta>This page continues the author’s natural philosophy regarding the heavens, specifically addressing the mechanical causes of orbital motion and the physical nature of the firmament.</meta>

299

->of this Treatise.<-

He gives the reason why each **celestial body** <term>celestial body: an astronomical object such as a planet or star</term> rotates invariably as if around an axis without deviating, and claims that this results only from the **initial motion** <note>original: "premier mouvement." This refers to the idea that God or a first cause set the universe in motion, and it continues by its own impetus.</note> that was imprinted upon it; just as a heavy mass set in motion and attached to a simple thread would always rotate evenly, provided the motion remained constant.

He concludes that the **superior waters** <note>These are the "waters above the firmament" mentioned in Genesis, which the author treats as a physical, albeit rarefied, substance.</note> do not wet, and claims that this is due to their extreme **rarefaction** <term>rarefaction: the state of being thin or less dense; in this context, it describes a substance becoming more spiritual or gaseous.</term>, and in passing he says that a learned **chemist** <note>original: "Chimiſte." At this time, chemistry and alchemy were often intertwined; the author suggests that understanding how substances change density is the most valuable part of the craft.</note> will derive more profit from the science of rarefaction than from any other science.

He examines the question of whether the **Firmament** or the "expanse" is composed of some matter, or if it is merely a **vacuum**; and he decides against the vacuum, determining that the **Firmament** is properly **air**, whose nature is much more suitable for **Light** than that of water.

<summary>This page explains the mechanics of celestial rotation as a result of initial motion and defines the firmament as a material substance (air) rather than a vacuum. It also highlights "rarefaction" as a key concept for both celestial physics and practical chemistry.</summary>
<keywords>celestial bodies, initial motion, superior waters, rarefaction, chemistry, firmament, vacuum, light, air</keywords>

### Summary

He says that to allow for **generations** <note>original: "generations." In this context, it refers to the ongoing creation, growth, and reproduction of all things in the natural world.</note>, God, after having separated the waters from the dry land or the earth, found it appropriate to create a **particular Light** destined for this task, which He placed in the **central fire** <term>central fire: an early geological and alchemical concept of a heat source at the heart of the Earth that drives natural processes like the growth of minerals</term>, and tempered this fire by the humidity of water and the coldness of the earth, in order to restrain its action, so that its heat might be more suitable to the design of its **Author** <note>A common historical reference to God as the creator or architect of the universe.</note>.

He says that this **central fire** acts continually upon the **humid matter** nearby, from which it causes a vapor to rise, which is the **Mercury of Nature** <note>In alchemy, the "Mercury of Nature" or "Philosophical Mercury" is the fluidic, transformative principle found in all things, thought to be the source of all metals.</note> and the **first matter** <term>first matter: or *prima materia*, the original, formless base of all substance from which everything is created</term> of the **three kingdoms** <note>The mineral, vegetable, and animal kingdoms.</note>.

He teaches that through the reaction of this central fire upon the **Mercurial vapor**, **Sulphur** <note>In the alchemical "Tria Prima" (Three Primes), Sulphur represents the "soul" or the active, combustible, and oily principle of matter.</note> is produced.

He also teaches that from the action of this fire upon the **aqueous humidity**, the salt called **Marine** <note>Sea salt; in this context, Salt is the third of the alchemical Primes, representing the "body," density, or stability of a substance.</note> is produced, when the **aerial humidity** <note>original: "humidité aërienne." This refers to the air-like or gaseous moisture trapped within the liquid.</note> enclosed within it happens to evaporate.

<summary>This page describes the alchemical origins of matter, explaining how a divinely placed "central fire" within the Earth interacts with moisture to produce the three primary principles: Mercury, Sulphur, and Salt.</summary>
<keywords>central fire, Mercury of Nature, Sulphur, Salt, three kingdoms, creation of matter, alchemy, cosmogony</keywords>
